(E.7)

Appendix F
Solution space of the boundary theory: the Yang-Mills case

Let
\sigma_{G}
:
T[1]\Sigma\to\widehat{E}
be a fixed (but generic) solution to the off-shell gravity boundary gPDE
(\widehat{E},Q)
in a “metric-like” gauge, as described in Section
5.3
.
In particular,

\sigma_{G}^{*}\widehat{\xi}^{A}=\theta^{A}
,
\sigma_{G}^{*}(\widehat{\lambda})=0
. As was discussed in this section, the choice of
\sigma_{G}
corresponds to the choice of a conformal metric on
\Sigma
together with the gravity subleading
\mathbf{T}_{AB}(x)
which is not relevant for the discussion of Yang-Mills.

In this appendix, we will focus on solutions of the gPDE
(\widehat{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{YM}},Q)
on a fixed background
\sigma_{G}
, i.e., sections
\sigma:T[1]\Sigma\to\widehat{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{YM}}
such that
\widehat{\pi}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{YM}}\circ\sigma=\sigma_{G}
17
17

17

Equivalently, they can be described as sections of the gPDE
(\widehat{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{YM}}|_{\sigma_{G}},Q,T[1]\Sigma)
.

From the action of
Q

\displaystyle Q\widehat{\mathcal{C}}=-\frac{1}{2}[\widehat{\mathcal{C}},\widehat{\mathcal{C}}]+\frac{1}{2}\,\widehat{\xi}^{A}\widehat{\xi}^{B}\widehat{F}_{AB}

(F.1)

it follows that

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\widehat{F}_{AB}=2\partial_{[A}A_{B]}+[A_{[A},A_{B]}].

(F.2)

where we introduced the notation
\sigma^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\equiv A_{B}(x)\theta^{B}
, and
A_{B}
is
\mathfrak{g}
-valued. Identifying
A_{B}
with the connection and introducing its curvature
F_{AB}[A]
, this formula simply means that
\sigma^{*}\widehat{F}_{AB}=F_{AB}[A]
.

Analogously to the gravitational case, we introduce the following vector field along the section:

\displaystyle\nabla_{C}^{g}\sigma^{*}f=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}}{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}\sigma^{*}f-\Gamma_{AC}^{B}(\sigma^{*}\Delta^{A}{}_{B}f)-A^{I}_{C}(\sigma^{*}R_{I}f)\,

(F.3)

, which is defined even when
\sigma_{YM}
is not a solution. Analogously to the gravitational case, such
\nabla_{C}^{g}
can, in a suitable coordinate system, be regarded as the Levi-Civita covariant derivative twisted by the connection
A
. We will adopt this interpretation henceforth.

However, using the fact that the action of
Q
on any degree
0
function can be decomposed as

\displaystyle Qf=\xi^{A}\nabla_{A}f+C_{A}{}^{B}\Delta^{A}{}_{B}f+\lambda^{A}\Gamma_{A}+\lambda\Delta f+\mathcal{C}^{I}R_{I}f

(F.4)

when
\sigma
is a solution, we find (see Section
5.3
)

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}\nabla_{C}f=\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}{\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}}{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}{\raisebox{0.5pt}{\footnotesize$\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{C}}$}\kern 1.0pt}f-\Gamma_{AC}^{B}(\sigma^{*}\Delta^{A}{}_{B}f)+P_{C}{}^{A}(\sigma^{*}\Gamma_{A}f)-A^{I}_{C}(\sigma^{*}R_{I}f)=\nabla_{C}^{g}\sigma^{*}f+P_{C}{}^{A}(\sigma^{*}\Gamma_{A}f)\,.

(F.5)

The formula for the Laplacian takes the same form as in (
5.44
).

\sigma^{*}\nabla_{A}\nabla^{A}f=\nabla_{A}^{g}\nabla^{g|A}\sigma^{*}f+(\partial_{A}P)\sigma^{*}\Gamma^{A}f+2P^{AD}\nabla^{g}_{A}\sigma^{*}\Gamma_{D}f+P^{AB}P_{A}{}^{C}\sigma^{*}\Gamma_{B}\Gamma_{C}f+\\
+P\sigma^{*}(-\Delta^{A}{}_{A}+\widehat{\Delta})f

(F.6)

Then, repeating the analysis of Section
5.3
we see that the space of solutions is parametrized by two off-shell
\mathfrak{g}
-valued fields:
A_{B}
and
\mathbf{J}_{A}(x)\equiv\sigma^{*}\mathcal{J}_{A}
. The on-shell conditions
\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}_{B}=\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}=0
will lead to differential equations on these fields.

In the
d=4
case (see Example
6.18
) using
\Gamma_{A}\widehat{F}_{BC}=0
,
\Gamma_{B}\mathcal{J}_{A}=\widetilde{\Lambda}\widehat{F}_{BA}
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}_{B}=\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B},\\
&\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}=\nabla_{A}^{g}\mathbf{J}^{A}.\end{split}

(F.7)

These are the Yang-Mills equation and the covariant conservation equation for the connection
A_{B}
and the current
\mathbf{J_{A}}
.

For the
d=6
case (see Example
6.19
) we need to note that from the boundary calculus Theorem
6.14
we have
\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{B}=\frac{\widetilde{\Lambda}}{D-5}\nabla^{A}\widehat{F}_{AB}
,
D>5
. Therefore, from formula (
6.57
) we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{D}\nabla^{A}\widehat{F}_{AB}=(D-5)\widehat{F}_{DB}.

(F.8)

Then, substituting the formula for Laplacian (
F.6
) into
\sigma^{*}\nabla_{C}\nabla^{C}\nabla^{A}\widehat{F}_{AB}
, we obtain from (
6.71
)
18
18

18

Recall that in Section
5.3
it was shown that
\sigma^{*}\widehat{C}_{ABC}=C_{ABC}[g]
,
\sigma^{*}\widehat{B}_{AB}=B_{AB}[g]
.

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{2}{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}_{B}=\Box^{g}\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B}+2\partial_{C}PF^{C}{}_{B}+4P^{AC}\nabla^{g}_{A}F_{CB}-3P\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B}+4C^{DA}{}_{B}F_{DA}-2[F_{AB},\nabla_{C}^{g}F^{CA}],\\
&\sigma^{*}{\mathcal{Y}}=\nabla_{A}^{g}\mathbf{J}^{A}+\frac{3}{2}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{2}P^{AC}(\nabla^{g}_{C}\nabla^{g|B}F_{BA}),\end{split}

(F.9)

where
\Box^{g}\equiv\nabla_{A}^{g}\nabla^{g|A}.

The first formula here is the Weyl-invariant generalization of the Yang-Mills equations, coinciding with the one appearing in
[
25
]
. The second is a modified conservation law for the current
\mathbf{J}^{A}
, which reduces to the standard conservation law, for example, when
F_{BA}=0
.

The case
d=8
(see Example
6.20
) is technically quite involved. We need to compute

\sigma^{*}\mathcal{Y}_{B}=\sigma^{*}\Big((\nabla_{A}\nabla^{A})\widehat{J}^{(3)}_{B}+\frac{3\widetilde{\Lambda^{2}}}{2}(\widehat{B}^{AC}\nabla_{C}\widehat{F}_{AB}-\widehat{F}_{CA}\nabla^{A}\widehat{B}_{B}{}^{C}+\frac{1}{2}\widehat{B}^{A}{}_{B}\nabla^{C}\widehat{F}_{CA})+\\
+6\widetilde{\Lambda}\widehat{C}^{AC}{}_{B}(2\nabla_{A}\widehat{J}_{C}^{(1)}-\nabla_{C}\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{A})+2[\widehat{J}^{(3)|A},\widehat{F}_{AB}]+6[\widehat{J}^{(1)|A},2\nabla_{A}\widehat{J}_{B}^{(1)}-\nabla_{B}\widehat{J}_{A}^{(1)}]\Big).

(F.10)

To begin with, we introduce the notation

\displaystyle j^{(1)}\equiv\frac{4}{\widetilde{\Lambda}}\sigma^{*}\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{B}\,,\hskip 9.24994ptj^{(3)}_{B}\equiv\frac{8}{3\widetilde{\Lambda}^{2}}\sigma^{*}\widehat{J}^{(3)}_{B}\,.

(F.11)

They can be computed in terms of the connection
A_{B}
similarly to (
F.7
), (
F.9
) (only the coefficients differ). Acting with
\sigma^{*}
on (
6.79
) we obtain the following:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&j^{(1)}_{B}=\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B},\\
&j^{(3)}_{B}=\Box^{g}\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B}+4\partial_{C}PF^{C}{}_{B}+8P^{AC}\nabla^{g}_{A}F_{CB}-3P\nabla^{g}_{A}F^{A}{}_{B}+8C^{DA}{}_{B}F_{DA}-2[F_{AB},\nabla_{C}^{g}F^{CA}]\,.\end{split}

(F.12)

To compute
\sigma^{*}\nabla_{A}\nabla^{A}J^{(3)}_{B}
using the formula for the Laplacian (
F.6
), we will need:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{D}\widehat{J}_{B}^{(3)}=3\widetilde{\Lambda}(2\nabla_{D}\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{B}-\nabla_{B}\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{D})\,,

(F.13)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{E}\Gamma_{D}\widehat{J}_{B}^{(3)}=3\widetilde{\Lambda}(\delta^{C}_{E}g_{BD}+\delta^{C}_{D}g_{EB}-5\delta^{C}_{B}g_{ED})\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{C}+3\widetilde{\Lambda}^{2}(2\nabla_{D}\widehat{F}_{EB}-\nabla_{B}\widehat{F}_{ED})\,,

(F.14)

where the first formula was obtained in (
E.3
), and the second can be derived from the first using the commutator formula (similarly to (
3.11
))
[\Gamma^{E},\nabla_{B}]=-\mathcal{P}^{EC}_{DB}\Delta^{D}{}_{C}+\delta^{E}_{B}\widehat{\Delta}
and
\Gamma_{E}\widehat{J}^{(1)}_{B}=\widetilde{\Lambda}\widehat{F}_{EB}
. Finally, putting all the pieces together,

\frac{8}{3\widetilde{\Lambda}^{2}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{Y}_{B}=\Box^{g}j^{(3)}_{B}+2(\partial^{D}P+2P^{AD}\nabla_{A}^{g})(2\nabla_{D}^{g}j^{(1)}_{B}-\nabla_{B}^{g}j^{(1)}_{D}+8P_{D}{}^{C}F_{CB}-4P_{B}{}^{C}F_{CD})+\\
+2(-5P_{AD}P^{AD}j^{(1)}_{B}+8P_{AE}P^{AD}\nabla^{g}_{D}F^{E}{}_{B}+2P_{AD}P^{A}{}_{B}j^{(1)|D})-5Pj^{(3)}_{B}+\\
+4(B^{AC}\nabla^{g}_{C}F_{AB}-F^{CA}(\nabla^{g}_{A}B_{BC}-4P^{E}{}_{A}C_{BEC}-4P^{E}{}_{A}C_{CEB})+\frac{1}{2}B^{A}{}_{B}\nabla^{g}_{C}F^{C}{}_{A})+\\
+4C^{AC}{}_{B}(2\nabla^{g}_{A}j_{C}^{(1)}-\nabla^{g}_{C}j^{(1)}_{A}+8P_{A}{}^{D}F_{DC}-4P_{C}{}^{D}F_{DA})+\\
+2[j^{(3)|A},F_{AB}]+[j^{(1)|A},2\nabla^{g}_{A}j_{B}^{(1)}-\nabla^{g}_{B}j_{A}^{(1)}+8P_{A}{}^{C}F_{CB}-4P_{B}{}^{C}F_{CA}]\,,

(F.15)

where we also used
\Gamma_{A}\widehat{B}_{BC}=-(D-5)(\widehat{C}_{CAB}+\widehat{C}_{BAC})
to calculate
\sigma^{*}\nabla_{A}\widehat{B}_{BC}
.

Similarly, the modified conservation law for
\mathbf{J}^{A}
can be computed:

\sigma^{*}\mathcal{Y}=\nabla^{g}_{A}\mathbf{J}^{A}+\frac{5\widetilde{\Lambda}^{3}}{4}P^{CB}(C_{C}{}^{D}{}_{B}j^{(1)}_{D}+B_{CD}F^{D}{}_{B}+3\nabla_{C}^{g}j_{B}^{(3)}+6P_{C}{}^{E}\nabla_{E}^{g}j^{(1)}_{B})+\\
+\frac{15\widetilde{\Lambda}^{3}}{16}B^{AC}(\nabla^{g}_{C}j^{(1)}_{A})-\frac{15\widetilde{\Lambda}^{3}}{32}[j^{(3)|A},j^{(1)}_{A}].

(F.16)
